\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

is equivalent to the
\Sigma
-type

\displaystyle\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Sigma$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(m\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({X},\hskip 1.5pt{{B}^{\prime}}))\,(i\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({X},\hskip 1.5pt{A}))\,(\gamma\mathrel{:}j\mathbin{\diamond}m=f\mathbin{\diamond}i)$}}\hskip 1.0pt,

\displaystyle(e_{{C}^{\prime}}\mathrel{:}{g}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}m=m_{{C}^{\prime}})\times(e_{A}\mathrel{:}i=m_{A})

\displaystyle\times\ ({\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}(\mathfrak{p}\mathbin{\ast}m)\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha\mathbin{\cdot}(g\mathbin{\ast}\gamma)\mathbin{\cdot}{\alpha}^{-1}=(k\mathbin{\ast}e_{{C}^{\prime}})\mathbin{\cdot}\xi\mathbin{\cdot}{(g\mathbin{\diamond}f\mathbin{\ast}e_{A})}^{-1}),

by the equality characterization of
Proposition

3.1.4
.
Contracting the singleton formed by the components
i
and
e_{A}
, this is equivalent to

\displaystyle\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Sigma$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(m\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({X},\hskip 1.5pt{{B}^{\prime}}))(e_{{C}^{\prime}}\mathrel{:}{g}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}m=m_{{C}^{\prime}})(e_{B}\mathrel{:}j\mathbin{\diamond}m=f\mathbin{\diamond}m_{A})$}}\hskip 1.0pt,

\displaystyle{\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}(\mathfrak{p}\mathbin{\ast}m)\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha=(k\mathbin{\ast}e_{{C}^{\prime}})\mathbin{\cdot}(\xi\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha)\mathbin{\cdot}{(g\mathbin{\ast}e_{B})}^{-1}.

But this type is also the fiber of the precomposition map
(\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}_{X}\_)
at the commuting square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{{C}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\mathbin{\diamond}m_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha}

obtained by “reparenthesizing” the diagram
\mathfrak{X}
.
Thus if
\mathfrak{P}
is a pullback (respectively, a weak pullback) then by its universal property (
Corollary

3.2.2
) the fiber
{\big(\frac{\textnormal{--}}{\mathfrak{P}}_{A,f,X}\big)}^{-1}(\mathfrak{X})
is contractible (respectively, pointed).

Conversely, for any
X\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
, we claim that the map

\displaystyle\varphi\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({X},\hskip 1.5pt{{B}^{\prime}})\to\mathrm{CommSq}_{(j,\mathrm{id}_{B})}(X)

\displaystyle\varphi(m)\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}(m,j\mathbin{\diamond}m,{\lambda_{j\mathbin{\diamond}m}}^{-1})

is an equivalence, and that the diagram of types and functions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CommSq}_{(j,\mathrm{id}_{B})}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}({X},\hskip 1.5pt{{B}^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CommSq}_{(k,g\mathbin{\diamond}\mathrm{id}_{B})}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CommSq}_{(k,g)}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{\textnormal{--}}{\mathfrak{P}}_{B,\mathrm{id}_{B},X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}_{X}\_}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

commutes, where
\psi
is the equivalence

(m_{{C}^{\prime}},m_{B},\gamma)\mapsto(m_{{C}^{\prime}},m_{B},\gamma\mathbin{\cdot}(\rho\mathbin{\ast}m_{B}))
.
That is,
(\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}_{X}\_)
is the pre- and post-composition of
\frac{\textnormal{--}}{\mathfrak{P}}_{B,\mathrm{id}_{B},X}
by equivalences.
Thus, if
\frac{\textnormal{--}}{\mathfrak{P}}
is a family of equivalences then so is
(\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}_{\_}\_)
, and if
\frac{\textnormal{--}}{\mathfrak{P}}
is a family of retractions then so is
(\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}_{\_}\_)
.

Now, the map
\varphi
is clearly a section of

\mathrm{fst}\mathrel{:}\mathrm{CommSq}_{(j,\mathrm{id})}(X)\to\mathcal{C}({X},\hskip 1.5pt{{B}^{\prime}})
.
By
Proposition

3.1.17
and transposition we see that it’s also a retraction of
\mathrm{fst}
, i.e. that

\varphi(m_{{B}^{\prime}})\equiv(m_{{B}^{\prime}},j\mathbin{\diamond}m_{{B}^{\prime}},{\lambda}^{-1})=(m_{{B}^{\prime}},m_{B},\gamma)

for all
m_{{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({X},\hskip 1.5pt{{B}^{\prime}})
,
m_{B}\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({X},\hskip 1.5pt{B})
and
\gamma\mathrel{:}j\mathbin{\diamond}m_{{B}^{\prime}}=\mathrm{id}\mathbin{\diamond}m_{B}
.

Finally, given
m\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({X},\hskip 1.5pt{{B}^{\prime}})
we calculate that

\mathfrak{S}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\textstyle\big(\psi\mathbin{\circ}\frac{\textnormal{--}}{\mathfrak{P}}_{B,\mathrm{id}_{B},X}\mathbin{\circ}\varphi\big)(m)

and

{\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}_{X}m

are commuting squares of type
\mathrm{CommSq}_{(k,g)(X)}
with the same morphism components
{g}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}m
and
j\mathbin{\diamond}m
.
The commutativity witness of
\mathfrak{S}
is

{\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}(\mathfrak{p}\mathbin{\ast}m)\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha\mathbin{\cdot}(g\mathbin{\ast}{\lambda}^{-1})\mathbin{\cdot}{\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}(\rho\mathbin{\ast}(j\mathbin{\diamond}m)),

while that of
{\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime}
is

{\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}(\mathfrak{p}\mathbin{\ast}m)\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha,

and these are equal since
(g\mathbin{\ast}{\lambda}^{-1})\mathbin{\cdot}{\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}(\rho\mathbin{\ast}(j\mathbin{\diamond}m))=\mathrm{refl}
by the triangle coherator.
∎

Lemma 3.2.7.

Vertical pullback pasting
.

Suppose we have a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{p}}

in a 2-coherent wild category
\mathcal{C}
.
Then if
\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}({g}^{\prime},j,\mathfrak{p})
is a pullback of
(k,g)
, the commuting square
\mathfrak{Q}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}({f}^{\prime},i,\mathfrak{q})
is a pullback of
(j,f)
if and only if the vertical pasting
\frac{\mathfrak{Q}}{\mathfrak{P}}
is a pullback of
(k,g\mathbin{\diamond}f)
.

Proof.

We claim that for any
X\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
, the triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CommSq}_{(j,f)}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}({X},\hskip 1.5pt{{A}^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{CommSq}_{(k,g\mathbin{\diamond}f)}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{\textnormal{--}}{\mathfrak{P}}_{A,f,X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Q}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}_{X}\_}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{\mathfrak{Q}}{\mathfrak{P}}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}_{X}\_}

commutes.
Then since
\mathfrak{P}
is a pullback, the map
\frac{\textnormal{--}}{\mathfrak{P}}_{A,f,X}
is an equivalence (
Lemma

3.2.6
), and it follows that
(\mathfrak{Q}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}\_)
is a family of equivalences if and only if
(\frac{\mathfrak{Q}}{\mathfrak{P}}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}\_)
is.

What remains is to construct a homotopy

\big(\frac{\mathfrak{Q}}{\mathfrak{P}}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}_{X}\_\big)=\big(\frac{\textnormal{--}}{\mathfrak{P}}_{A,f,X}\big)\mathbin{\circ}(\mathfrak{Q}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}_{X}\_)

for any
X
, i.e. a witness that, for any
m\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({X},\hskip 1.5pt{{A}^{\prime}})
, the commuting squares

\frac{\mathfrak{Q}}{\mathfrak{P}}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}_{X}m\hskip 4.30554pt\equiv\hskip 4.30554pt\left(({g}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}{f}^{\prime})\mathbin{\diamond}m,\ i\mathbin{\diamond}m,\ {\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}\big(\textstyle\frac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbin{\ast}m\big)\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha\right)

and

\frac{\mathfrak{Q}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}_{X}m}{\mathfrak{P}}\hskip 4.30554pt\equiv\hskip 4.30554pt\Big({g}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}{f}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}m,\ i\mathbin{\diamond}m,\ \frac{{\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}(\mathfrak{q}\mathbin{\ast}m)\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha}{\mathfrak{p}}\Big)

are equal.
By
Proposition

3.1.4
together with the canonical equalities
\alpha\mathrel{:}({g}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}{f}^{\prime})\mathbin{\diamond}m={g}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}{f}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}m
and
\mathrm{refl}\mathrel{:}i\mathbin{\diamond}m=i\mathbin{\diamond}m
, it’s enough to show that

{\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}\big(\textstyle\frac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbin{\ast}m\big)\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha=(k\mathbin{\ast}\alpha)\mathbin{\cdot}\frac{{\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}(\mathfrak{q}\mathbin{\ast}m)\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha}{\mathfrak{p}}.

Diagram 3.2.7.1:
Construction of

{\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}\big(\textstyle\frac{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbin{\ast}m\big)\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha=(k\mathbin{\ast}\alpha)\mathbin{\cdot}\frac{{\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}(\mathfrak{q}\mathbin{\ast}m)\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha}{\mathfrak{p}}
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{k\mathbin{\diamond}({g}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}{f}^{\prime})\mathbin{\diamond}m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{k\mathbin{\diamond}{g}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}{f}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(k\mathbin{\diamond}{g}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}{f}^{\prime})\mathbin{\diamond}m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(k\mathbin{\diamond}{g}^{\prime})\mathbin{\diamond}{f}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((k\mathbin{\diamond}{g}^{\prime})\mathbin{\diamond}{f}^{\prime})\mathbin{\diamond}m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(g\mathbin{\diamond}j)\mathbin{\diamond}{f}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((g\mathbin{\diamond}j)\mathbin{\diamond}{f}^{\prime})\mathbin{\diamond}m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g\mathbin{\diamond}j\mathbin{\diamond}{f}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(g\mathbin{\diamond}j\mathbin{\diamond}{f}^{\prime})\mathbin{\diamond}m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g\mathbin{\diamond}(j\mathbin{\diamond}{f}^{\prime})\mathbin{\diamond}m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(g\mathbin{\diamond}f\mathbin{\diamond}i)\mathbin{\diamond}m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g\mathbin{\diamond}(f\mathbin{\diamond}i)\mathbin{\diamond}m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((g\mathbin{\diamond}f)\mathbin{\diamond}i)\mathbin{\diamond}m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g\mathbin{\diamond}f\mathbin{\diamond}i\mathbin{\diamond}m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(g\mathbin{\diamond}f)\mathbin{\diamond}i\mathbin{\diamond}m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k\mathbin{\ast}\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\alpha}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\alpha}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\ast}m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{p}\mathbin{\ast}({f}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}m)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathfrak{p}\mathbin{\ast}{f}^{\prime})\mathbin{\ast}m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\mathbin{\ast}m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g\mathbin{\ast}{\alpha}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(g\mathbin{\ast}\mathfrak{q})\mathbin{\ast}m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g\mathbin{\ast}(\mathfrak{q}\mathbin{\ast}m)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\ast}m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g\mathbin{\ast}\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\alpha}^{-1}}

With a little path algebra (noting
Proposition

2.1.4
) this amounts to showing commutativity of the outer boundary of
Diagram

3.2.7.1
.
By inserting associators
\alpha
as shown in the interior of the diagram, we decompose the outer shape into a pasting of three commuting pentagons (by the pentagonators) and two commuting squares (by
Proposition

2.1.4
).
Thus the entire diagram commutes.
∎

Corollary 3.2.8.

Horizontal pullback pasting
.

Since the transpose of a pullback is a pullback (
Proposition

3.2.3
), by taking transposes as appropriate we deduce the more familiar horizontal version of the pullback pasting lemma.

Lemma 3.2.9.

Pullback prism
.

Suppose we have a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{{B}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{A}}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

in a 2-coherent wild category
\mathcal{C}
, such that
c\mathrel{:}g\mathbin{\diamond}h={g}^{\prime}
is a commuting triangle,

\mathfrak{p}\mathrel{:}f\mathbin{\diamond}\pi_{A}=g\mathbin{\diamond}\pi_{B}
and
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}\mathrel{:}f\mathbin{\diamond}{\pi_{A}}^{\prime}={g}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}\pi_{{B}^{\prime}}
,
and where the squares

\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}(\pi_{A},\pi_{B},\mathfrak{p})\mathrel{:}\mathrm{CommSq}_{(f,g)}(P)

and
{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}({\pi_{A}}^{\prime},\pi_{{B}^{\prime}},{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime})\mathrel{:}\mathrm{CommSq}_{(f,{g}^{\prime})}({P}^{\prime})

are both pullbacks.
Then there is a contractible type of data consisting of:

❂

a morphism
m\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({{P}^{\prime}},\hskip 1.5pt{P})
,

❂

equalities

e\mathrel{:}\pi_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}m={\pi_{A}}^{\prime}

and
\mathfrak{q}\mathrel{:}\pi_{B}\mathbin{\diamond}m=h\mathbin{\diamond}\pi_{{B}^{\prime}}
completing the boundary of the prism, and

❂

an equality 3-cell
\eta
filling the volume of the completed prism.

Even more, the top face
(m,\pi_{{B}^{\prime}},\mathfrak{q})
of the completed prism is a pullback of
(\pi_{B},h)
.

Proof.

From the universal property of
\mathfrak{P}
we get
m
,
e
,
\mathfrak{q}
and the equality

\eta\mathrel{:}{\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}(\mathfrak{p}\mathbin{\ast}m)\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha=(f\mathbin{\ast}e)\mathbin{\cdot}{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\cdot}({c}^{-1}\mathbin{\ast}\pi_{{B}^{\prime}})\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha\mathbin{\cdot}{(g\mathbin{\ast}\mathfrak{q})}^{-1}

as the center of contraction of the fiber of
(\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}_{{P}^{\prime}}\_)
at the commuting square

\mathfrak{S}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\big({\pi_{A}}^{\prime},\ h\mathbin{\diamond}\pi_{{B}^{\prime}},\ {\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\cdot}({c}^{-1}\mathbin{\ast}\pi_{{B}^{\prime}})\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha\big)

on
(f,g)
.
Let
\mathfrak{Q}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}(m,\pi_{{B}^{\prime}},\mathfrak{q})
; then by
\eta
and
Lemma

3.1.16
it follows that
(f,g\mathbin{\diamond}h,\frac{\mathfrak{Q}}{\mathfrak{P}})
and
(f,{g}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime})
are equal commuting squares on
{P}^{\prime}
,
A
,
{B}^{\prime}
,
C
.
Since
{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}
is a pullback, by transport so is
\frac{\mathfrak{Q}}{\mathfrak{P}}
, and by pullback pasting (
Lemma

3.2.7
) so too is
\mathfrak{Q}
.
∎

3.3.  The truncation level of pullbacks

Definition 3.3.1.

Pullbacks on a cospan
.

As observed earlier, the predicate

\mathrm{is\text{-}pullback}_{\mathfrak{c},\,P}(\mathfrak{P})\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Pi$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(X\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0})$}}\;{\mathrm{is\text{-}equiv}\,(\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}_{X}\_)}

on
\mathrm{CommSq}_{\mathfrak{c}}(P)
is propositional.
We obtain the subtype of pullbacks on a cospan
\mathfrak{c}
, with fixed source

\mathrm{Pullback}_{\mathfrak{c}}(P)\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Sigma$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(\mathfrak{P}\mathrel{:}\mathrm{CommSq}_{\mathfrak{c}}(P))$}}{\,{\mathrm{is\text{-}pullback}_{\mathfrak{c},\,P}(\mathfrak{P})}},

and with arbitrary source,

\mathrm{Pullback}(\mathfrak{c})\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Sigma$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$((P,\mathfrak{P})\mathrel{:}\mathrm{CommSq}(\mathfrak{c}))$}}{\,{\mathrm{is\text{-}pullback}_{\mathfrak{c},\,P}(\mathfrak{P})}}.

Proposition 3.3.2.

\mathrm{Pullback}(\mathfrak{c})
is a set in set-level categories
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is set-level then
\mathrm{Pullback}(\mathfrak{c})
is a
\Sigma
-type of sets and propositions for any cospan
\mathfrak{c}
, and thus also a set.

Proposition 3.3.3.

\mathrm{Pullback}(\mathfrak{c})
is a proposition in univalent 2-coherent wild categories
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a univalent 2-coherent wild category, then
\mathrm{Pullback}(\mathfrak{c})
is a proposition for any cospan
\mathfrak{c}
.

Proof.

In summary, by univalence and the universal property of pullbacks.
Suppose that
(P,\mathfrak{P})
and
({P}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime})
are elements of
\mathrm{Pullback}(\mathfrak{c})
.
Then
{(\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}\_)}
and
({\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}\_)
are equivalences, and from the centers of contraction of

{(\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}\_)}^{-1}({\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime})

and

{({\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}\_)}^{-1}(\mathfrak{P})

we get
m\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({P},\hskip 1.5pt{{P}^{\prime}})
and
{m}^{\prime}\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({{P}^{\prime}},\hskip 1.5pt{P})
such that

e\mathrel{:}{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}m=\mathfrak{P}\hskip 6.45831pt\text{and}\hskip 6.45831pt{e}^{\prime}\mathrel{:}\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}{m}^{\prime}={\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}.

Furthermore, by
Lemma

3.1.10

\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}({m}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}m)\hskip 4.30554pt{\mathrel{\to}}\hskip 4.30554pt(\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}{m}^{\prime})\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}m\hskip 4.30554pt{\mathrel{\to}}\hskip 4.30554pt{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}m\hskip 4.30554pt{\mathrel{\to}}\hskip 4.30554pt\mathfrak{P},

and so
{m}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}m=\mathrm{id}_{P}
by contractibility of
{(\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}\_)}^{-1}(\mathfrak{P})
and
Lemma

3.1.10
again.

By a similar argument
m\mathbin{\diamond}{m}^{\prime}=\mathrm{id}_{{P}^{\prime}}
, and so
m\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({P},\hskip 1.5pt{{P}^{\prime}})
is a
\mathcal{C}
-equivalence.
From univalence of
\mathcal{C}
we now get an equality

\mathrm{eqvtoid}_{\mathcal{C}}(m)\mathrel{:}P={P}^{\prime},

with

\mathfrak{P}\hskip 4.30554pt{\mathrel{\to}}\hskip 4.30554pt{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}m\hskip 4.30554pt{\mathrel{\to}}\hskip 4.30554pt{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}\mathrm{idd}(\mathrm{eqvtoid}_{\mathcal{C}}(m)).

By
Corollary

3.1.13
, this proves
(P,\mathfrak{P})=({P}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime})
.
∎

4.  Wild Categories with Families

We can now define precoherent higher internal models of homotopical MLTT.
We begin by simply taking Dybjer’s generalized algebraic definition of a category with families
[
Dyb96
]
, and allowing contexts to form wild categories.
This notion has previously been briefly considered by Kraus
[
Kra21
, Definition 5]
.

4.1.  Typed term structures

Definition 4.1.1.

Typed term structures on wild categories
.

Let
\mathcal{U}
be a universe and
\mathcal{C}
a wild category.
A
typed term structure
on
\mathcal{C}
(valued in
\mathcal{U}
) consists of the following data:

❂

A wild
\mathcal{U}
-valued presheaf of
\mathcal{C}
-types
over
\mathcal{C}
, presented as a generalized algebraic theory by the components
11
11

11

Implicitly quantifying over objects
\Gamma,\Delta,\mathrm{E}\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
as needed.

\displaystyle\mathsf{Ty}

\displaystyle\mathrel{:}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{0}\to\mathcal{U}

\displaystyle\_{\mathsf{[}{\_}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{:}

\displaystyle\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta\to\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta})\to\mathsf{Ty}\,\Gamma

and equations
Footnote

11
expressing functoriality

\displaystyle{{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{:}

\displaystyle A{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}=A

\displaystyle\hskip 6.45831pt\text{for all}\hskip 6.45831ptA\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Gamma

\displaystyle{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{:}

\displaystyle A{\mathsf{[}{\tau\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}=A{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 6.45831pt\text{for all}\hskip 6.45831ptA\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\mathrm{E},\ \sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta}),\ \tau\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Delta},\hskip 1.5pt{\mathrm{E}}).

❂

A wild
\mathcal{U}
-valued presheaf of
\mathcal{C}
-terms
over the (wild) category of elements of the
\mathcal{C}
-type presheaf, presented
Footnote

11
by

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tm}

\displaystyle\mathrel{:}

\displaystyle(\Gamma\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0})\to\mathsf{Ty}\,\Gamma\to\mathcal{U}

\displaystyle\_{\mathsf{[}{\_}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{:}

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tm}_{\,{\Delta}}\,A\to(\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta}))\to\mathsf{Tm}_{\,{\Gamma}}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})

\displaystyle\hskip 6.45831pt\text{for all}\hskip 6.45831ptA\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta

and

\displaystyle{{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{:}

\displaystyle a{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}=a{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}_{\,{\Gamma}}}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 6.45831pt\text{for all}\hskip 6.45831ptA\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Gamma,\ a\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Tm}_{\,{\Gamma}}\,A

\displaystyle{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{:}

\displaystyle a{\mathsf{[}{\tau\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}=a{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}_{\,{\Gamma}}}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 6.45831pt\text{for all}\hskip 6.45831pt\begin{aligned} A&\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\mathrm{E},\ a\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Tm}_{\,{\mathrm{E}}}\,A\\
\sigma&\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta}),\ \tau\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Delta},\hskip 1.5pt{\mathrm{E}}).\end{aligned}

The actions
\_{\mathsf{[}{\_}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}
and
\_{\mathsf{[}{\_}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}
of the type and term presheaves on morphisms are called
substitution in types
and
substitution in terms
, respectively.

We will often denote a typed term structure on a wild category simply by the object parts of its component presheaves
(\mathsf{Ty},\mathsf{Tm})
.
We also frequently elide the first argument of
\mathsf{Tm}
and write, for example,
\mathsf{Tm}\,A
instead of
\mathsf{Tm}_{\,{\Gamma}}\,A
.

Note the following equivalences and equalities in typed term structures
(\mathsf{Ty},\mathsf{Tm})
on wild categories
\mathcal{C}
.

Proposition 4.1.2.

For every
\Gamma\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
and
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Gamma
, the equation
{{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}
(
Definition

4.1.1
) implies that the function

\displaystyle\_{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}

\displaystyle\ \mathrel{:}\ \

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tm}\,A

\displaystyle\to\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle\mapsto a{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}

is equal to transport in
\mathsf{Tm}_{\,{\Gamma}}
along
{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}
, and is hence an equivalence.

Definition 4.1.3.

Assume objects
\Gamma,\Delta\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
, a
\mathcal{C}
-type
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta
, and an equality
e\mathrel{:}\sigma=\tau
of morphisms
\sigma,\tau\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta})
.
We write

{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{e}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\mathrm{ap}\,{(A{\mathsf{[}{\_}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})}\,{e}

for the induced equality
A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}=A{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}
.
By induction on
e
, we also have an equality

{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{e}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}\mathrel{:}{a{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{e}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}=a{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}

for any
\mathcal{C}
-term
a\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Tm}\,A
.

By
[
Uni13
, Lemma 2.2.2]
,
{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{\_}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
respects trivial, composite and inverse equalities.
Furthermore,

Proposition 4.1.4.

For any
\Gamma,\Delta\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
,
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta
,
a\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})
and morphisms
\sigma,\tau\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta})
such that
e\mathrel{:}\sigma=\tau
,

a{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\_}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})}_{\,{e}}}=a{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{e}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}

by
[
Uni13
, Lemma 2.3.10]
.

Definition 4.1.5.

Suppose that
A,{A}^{\prime}\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta
are
\mathcal{C}
-types such that
e\mathrel{:}A={A}^{\prime}
.
For any
\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta})
, we write

{e}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\mathrm{ap}\,{(\_{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})}\,{e}

for the induced equality
A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}={A}^{\prime}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}
.

Similarly, if
a,{a}^{\prime}\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Tm}\,A
with
e\mathrel{:}a={a}^{\prime}
, we write

{e}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\mathrm{ap}\,{(\_{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}})}\,{e}

for the induced equality
a{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}={a}^{\prime}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}
.

Proposition 4.1.6.

Substitution in transported terms
.

If
e\mathrel{:}A=_{\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta}{A}^{\prime}
,
then for any
a\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Tm}\,A
and morphism
\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta})
,

(a{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{e}}}){\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}={a{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{{e}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}

by induction on
e
.

Proposition 4.1.7.

{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
is a natural isomorphism
.

Suppose that
\sigma,{\sigma}^{\prime}\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta})
are morphisms such that
e\mathrel{:}\sigma={\sigma}^{\prime}
.
By induction on
e
, we have that
the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{\tau\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{\tau\mathbin{\diamond}{\sigma}^{\prime}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{{\sigma}^{\prime}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{\tau\mathbin{\ast}e}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{e}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}

canonically commutes for all
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\mathrm{E}
and
\tau\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Delta},\hskip 1.5pt{\mathrm{E}})
, and also that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{{\sigma}^{\prime}\mathbin{\diamond}\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{{\sigma}^{\prime}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{e\mathbin{\ast}\varrho}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{e}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}{\mathsf{[}{\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}

canonically commutes for all
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta
and
\varrho\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\mathrm{B}},\hskip 1.5pt{\Gamma})
.

The following two definitions are analogous to the conditions for a pseudofunctor between weak (2, 1)-categories (i.e. bicategories where all 2-cells are invertible).
12
12

12

See e.g.
[
JY21
, §4.1]
.

In the case that
\mathcal{C}
is 2-coherent, they improve the wild presheaf
\mathsf{Ty}
of a typed term structure on
\mathcal{C}
to what might be called a
wild weak (2, 1)-presheaf
.

Definition 4.1.8.

Type triangulators
.

A typed term structure on a wild category
\mathcal{C}
is said to have
type triangulators
if for all morphisms
\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta})
and
\mathcal{C}
-types
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta
the following triangles commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{id}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{\lambda}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\mathrm{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{\rho}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}

Definition 4.1.9.

Type pentagonators
.

A typed term structure on a wild category
\mathcal{C}
has
type pentagonators
if for all morphisms

\Gamma\xrightarrow{\varrho}\Delta\xrightarrow{\sigma}\mathrm{E}\xrightarrow{\tau}\mathrm{Z}

and
\mathcal{C}
-types
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\mathrm{Z}
, the following pentagon commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{\tau\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{(\tau\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma)\mathbin{\diamond}\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{\tau\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{{\alpha}^{-1}}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}{\mathsf{[}{\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}

4.2.  Context extension structures and wild cwfs

Definition 4.2.1.

Context extension structures
.

Assume a typed term structure
(\mathsf{Ty},\mathsf{Tm})
on a wild category
\mathcal{C}
.
A
context extension structure
on
(\mathcal{C},\mathsf{Ty},\mathsf{Tm})
is given
13
13

13

Again, implicitly generalizing over
\Gamma,\Delta,\mathrm{E}\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
as needed.

by the following components

\displaystyle\_\hskip 1.0pt\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0pt\_

\displaystyle\mathrel{:}

\displaystyle(\Gamma\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0})\to\mathsf{Ty}\,\Gamma\to{\mathcal{C}}_{0}

\displaystyle\mathsf{p}

\displaystyle\mathrel{:}

\displaystyle(A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Gamma)\to\mathcal{C}({\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA},\hskip 1.5pt{\Gamma})

\displaystyle\mathsf{q}

\displaystyle\mathrel{:}

\displaystyle(A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Gamma)\to\mathsf{Tm}_{\,{\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA}}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{p}_{A}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})

\displaystyle\_\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\_

\displaystyle\mathrel{:}

\displaystyle(\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta}))\to\mathsf{Tm}_{\,{\Gamma}}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})\to\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA})

\displaystyle\hskip 6.45831pt\text{for all}\hskip 6.45831ptA\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta

and equations (note
Definition

4.1.3
)

\displaystyle\mathsf{p}\upbeta

\displaystyle\mathrel{:}

\displaystyle\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}(\sigma\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pta)=\sigma

and

\displaystyle\mathsf{q}\upbeta

\displaystyle\mathrel{:}

\displaystyle\mathsf{q}_{A}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pta}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}=a{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{{{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{\mathsf{p}\upbeta}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 6.45831pt\text{for all}\hskip 6.45831pt\begin{aligned} \sigma&\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta}),\\
A&\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta,\ a\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Tm}_{\,{\Gamma}}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\upeta

\displaystyle\mathrel{:}

\displaystyle(\mathsf{p}_{A}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}_{A})=\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA}

\displaystyle\hskip 6.45831pt\text{for all}\hskip 6.45831ptA\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathbin{\diamond}

\displaystyle\mathrel{:}

\displaystyle(\tau\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pta)\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma=({\tau\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt{{a{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}}})

\displaystyle\hskip 6.45831pt\text{for all}\hskip 6.45831pt\begin{aligned} \sigma&\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta}),\ \tau\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Delta},\hskip 1.5pt{\mathrm{E}}),\\
A&\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\mathrm{E},\ a\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Tm}_{\,{\Delta}}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}).\end{aligned}

We call
\mathsf{p}
the
display map
, and
\mathsf{q}
the
generic term
of the context extension structure.

We sometimes elide the argument
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Gamma
to the display map
\mathsf{p}
and the generic term
\mathsf{q}
of a context extension structure.
When we need to be concise, we denote the display map
\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA\xrightarrow{\mathsf{p}_{A}}\Gamma
by
\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma
.

Definition 4.2.2.

Cwf structures on wild categories
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a wild category, a
cwf structure
on
\mathcal{C}
consists of:

❂

a terminal object
\text{\char 12\relax}\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
,

❂

a typed term structure
(\mathsf{Ty},\mathsf{Tm})
on
\mathcal{C}
, and

❂

a context extension structure on
(\mathcal{C},\mathsf{Ty},\mathsf{Tm})
.

These model the structural rules of a Martin-Löf type theory over
\mathcal{C}
.

Definition 4.2.3.

Wild categories with families
.

A
wild category with families (wild cwf)
is a wild category
\mathcal{C}
together with a cwf structure on
\mathcal{C}
.
In this case, we call
\mathcal{C}
the
category of contexts
of the wild cwf, its objects
contexts
, and its morphisms
substitutions
.

We usually denote a wild cwf by its category of contexts.

Of course, every 1-cwf is a wild cwf.

Example 4.2.4.

Universe cwfs
.

If a universe wild category
\mathcal{U}
has
\Sigma
-types that satisfy the
\upeta
-rule, then it supports a canonical wild cwf structure given as follows.

❂

The terminal context
ﬁ
is the unit type
\mathbb{1}\mathrel{:}\mathcal{U}
.

❂

The typed term structure is as follows.

\mathcal{U}
-types in context
\Gamma
are
\Gamma
-indexed type families

\displaystyle\mathsf{Ty}\mathrel{:}\mathcal{U}\to{\mathcal{U}}^{+}

\displaystyle\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\Delta\to\mathcal{U},

while
\mathcal{U}
-terms
a\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Tm}_{\,{\Delta}}\,A
are sections of
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta

\mathsf{Tm}_{\,{\Delta}}\,A\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\Pi\,{\Delta}\,{A}.

Substitution of
\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{U}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta})
in
\mathcal{U}
-types
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta
and
\mathcal{U}
-terms
a
is given by precomposition

\displaystyle A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}A\mathbin{\circ}\sigma,

\displaystyle a{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}a\mathbin{\circ}\sigma.

This action is definitionally functorial—that is,
{{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
,
{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
,
{{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}
and
{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}
are all families of trivial identities.

❂

The context extension structure is given by dependent pairing.
The extended context
\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA
is
\Sigma\,{\Delta}{\,{A}}
, and
\mathsf{p}
and
\mathsf{q}
are the functions
\mathrm{fst}
and
\mathrm{snd}
respectively.
For
\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{U}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta})
and
t\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Tm}_{\,{\Gamma}}\,(A\mathbin{\circ}\sigma)
, the extended substitution
(\sigma\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0ptt)\mathrel{:}\mathcal{U}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Sigma\,{\Delta}{\,{A}}})
is given by

(\sigma\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0ptt)(\gamma)\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}(\sigma(\gamma),t(\gamma)).

Again, the equations for context extension structures hold definitionally.
In particular, the
\upeta
-rule for
\Sigma
-types is used for
\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\upeta
.

We refer to the resulting wild cwf as the
universe cwf
.

Variations of this canonical universe cwf structure appear throughout the literature as the “standard model”.

Example 4.2.5.

Subuniverse cwfs and the 1-cwf of sets
.

The construction of the typed term and context extension structures of
Example

4.2.4
works equally well for any
sub
universe wild category (
Examples

2.0.2
) that has a terminal object and is closed under
\Sigma
-types with
\upeta
.
In particular, the “1-cwf” of sets
\mathrm{Set}_{\mathcal{U}}
is a subuniverse cwf of
\mathcal{U}
.

Definition 4.2.6.

Univalent wild cwfs
.

A wild cwf
\mathcal{C}
is called
univalent
if its category of contexts is univalent.

Example 4.2.7.

Univalent wild cwfs
.

Any subuniverse of a univalent universe
\mathcal{U}
yields a univalent cwf.
In particular,
\mathrm{Set}_{\mathcal{U}}
and
\mathcal{U}
are univalent cwfs if
\mathcal{U}
is a univalent universe.

4.3.  Structural properties of wild cwfs

From now on we assume that
\mathcal{C}
is a wild cwf.

Lemma 4.3.1.

Substitutions into extended contexts are pairs
.

Let
\Gamma,\Delta\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
be contexts, and
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta
a
\mathcal{C}
-type.
There is an equivalence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Sigma$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta}))$}}\hskip 1.0pt,{\,{\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})}},}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma\ \mapsto\ (\mathsf{p}_{A}\,\mathbin{\diamond}\,\sigma\,,\ {\mathsf{q}_{A}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\sigma\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pta)\ \mapsfrom\ (\sigma,\,a)}

where the reverse function sends a pair
(\sigma,a)
to the extended substitution
(\sigma\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pta)
given by the context extension structure (
Definition

4.2.1
).

Proof.

For one composition, it’s enough to show that for all
\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA})
,

(\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt{{\mathsf{q}_{A}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}}})\hskip 4.30554pt{\mathrel{\to}^{{\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathbin{\diamond}}^{-1}}}\hskip 4.30554pt(\mathsf{p}_{A}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}_{A})\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma\hskip 4.30554pt{\mathrel{\to}^{\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\upeta\mathbin{\ast}\sigma}}\hskip 4.30554pt\mathrm{id}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma\hskip 4.30554pt{\mathrel{\to}^{\lambda}}\hskip 4.30554pt\sigma.

For the other, we show the equality of pairs

(\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}(\sigma\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pta),{\mathsf{q}_{A}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pta}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}})=(\sigma,a).

Equality of the first components is given by
\mathsf{p}\upbeta
(
Definition

4.2.1
), and for the second components we have that

\begin{aligned} b]{2}&{\mathsf{q}_{A}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pta}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\_}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})}_{\,{\mathsf{p}\upbeta}}}\\
\hskip 4.30554pt{\mathrel{\to}}\hskip 4.30554pt&{\mathsf{q}_{A}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pta}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{\mathsf{p}\upbeta}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}&\text{(by \lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{prop:transport-in-Tm-of-substT})}\\
\hskip 4.30554pt{\mathrel{\to}}\hskip 4.30554pt&a\hskip 30.1388pt&\text{(by $\mathsf{p}\upbeta$ and properties of transport)}\end{aligned}.\qed

Corollary 4.3.2.

Elimination principle for
\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0pt{A}})
.

By
Lemma

4.3.1
, to construct a section of a family of types
P
over
\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0pt{A}})
, it’s enough to give an element of
P((\sigma\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pta))
for every
\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta})
and
a\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})
.

Corollary 4.3.3.

Equality of substitutions into extended contexts
.

If
\sigma
and
\tau
are substitutions from
\Gamma
to
\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA
, then by
Lemma

4.3.1
, the fact that equivalences induce equivalent identity types
[
Uni13
, Theorem 2.11.1]
, and
Proposition

4.1.4
, an equality
\sigma=\tau
is equivalent to a pair of equalities

e\mathrel{:}\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma=\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\diamond}\tau\hskip 6.45831pt\text{and}\hskip 6.45831pt\mathsf{q}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{e}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}=\mathsf{q}{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}.

An alternative but equivalent
formulation is the following—for substitutions of the form
(\sigma,a),(\tau,b)\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA})
,

\big((\sigma,a)=_{\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA})}(\tau,b)\big)\ \simeq\ \big(\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Sigma$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(e\mathrel{:}\sigma=\tau)$}}\hskip 1.0pt,{\,{a{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{e}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}=b}}\big).

To see this, write
\varphi
for the forward equivalence of
Lemma

4.3.1
.
In the proof of
Lemma

4.3.1
we showed that the equalities of pairs
\varphi((\sigma,a))=(\sigma,a)
and
\varphi((\tau,b))=(\tau,b)
hold.
Then
\varphi((\sigma,a))=\varphi((\tau,b))
is equivalent to the equality type of pairs
(\sigma,a)=(\tau,b)
, which by
Proposition

4.1.4
is equivalent to the
\Sigma
-type as claimed.

Lemma 4.3.4.

Terms are sections of display maps
.

For all contexts
\Gamma\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
and
\mathcal{C}
-types
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Gamma
, there is an equivalence

\mathsf{Tm}\,A\simeq\mathrm{Sect}(\mathsf{p}_{A})

whose forward map sends the
\mathcal{C}
-term
a
to the section
(\mathrm{id}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pta{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}})
of
\mathsf{p}_{A}
, witnessed by
\mathsf{p}\upbeta
.

Proof.

We have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sect}(\mathsf{p}_{A})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Sigma$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA}))$}}\hskip 1.0pt,{\,{\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma=\mathrm{id}}}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Sigma$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(u\mathrel{:}\Sigma\,{(\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Gamma}))},{\,{\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})}})$}}\hskip 1.0pt,{\,{\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}(\mathrm{fst}\,u\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathrm{snd}\,u)=\mathrm{id}}}

(by
Lemma

4.3.1
)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Sigma$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(u\mathrel{:}\Sigma\,{(\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Gamma}))},{\,{\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})}})$}}\hskip 1.0pt,{\,{\mathrm{fst}\,u=\mathrm{id}}}

(by
\mathsf{p}\upbeta
(
Definition

4.2.1
))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Sigma$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(u\mathrel{:}\Sigma\,{(\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Gamma}))},{\,{\sigma=\mathrm{id}}})$}}\hskip 1.0pt,{\,{\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{fst}\,u}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})}}

(assoc. of
\Sigma
and comm. of
\times
)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})

(contractibility of singletons)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tm}\,A

(by the inverse of the equivalence
\_{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}
(
Proposition

4.1.2
)).

Tracing the composition of this chain of equivalences, we compute that its inverse is equal to the map

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tm}\,A

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{Sect}(\mathsf{p}_{A})

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle\mapsto((\mathrm{id}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pta{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}),\mathsf{p}\upbeta).\qed

Analogues of
Lemmas

4.3.1
and
4.3.4
were already observed for set-level 1-cwfs by Dybjer
[
Dyb96
]
and by Hofmann
[
Hof97
]
in a traditional 1-categorical setting.
In that setting, these properties essentially follow from the fact that context extension structures on
(\mathcal{C},\mathsf{Ty},\mathsf{Tm})
are choices of representing objects for particular presheaves on slices of
\mathcal{C}
.

It may seem slightly surprising that the fully coherent homotopical versions of the same properties hold for arbitrary, even noncoherent, wild cwfs.
On the other hand, given that a context extension structure for 1-cwfs essentially spells out the universal property of representability of a certain presheaf (and, relatedly, that the equivalent natural models
[
Awo18
]
have a relatively simple axiomatization in terms of representability of pullbacks of presheaves), it is perhaps to be expected that certain consequences would carry over immediately to higher generalizations even without imposing additional coherence conditions.

5.  2-Coherence for Context Extension

We now construct and analyze in more detail a characterization of the equality of substitutions into extended contexts.

First, consider the case where
\sigma
and
\tau
are substitutions from
\Gamma
to
arbitrary
contexts
\Delta
, with
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta
,
a\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})
and
b\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})
.
From the equivalence

\big(\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Sigma$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta}))$}}\hskip 1.0pt,{\,{\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})}}\big)\xrightarrow[(\_\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\_)]{\sim}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0pt{A}})

of
Lemma

4.3.1
, we obtain

\big((\sigma,a)=_{\Sigma\,{(\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA}))}{\,{(\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\_}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}))}}}(\tau,b)\big)\xrightarrow[\mathrm{ap}\,{(\_\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\_)}]{\sim}\big((\sigma\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pta)=_{\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA})}(\tau\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0ptb)\big).

Precomposing this with

\displaystyle\big(\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Sigma$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(e\mathrel{:}\sigma=\tau)$}}\hskip 1.0pt,{\,{a{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{e}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}=b}}\big)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\mathrm{id}\,\times\,\varphi]{\sim}\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Sigma$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(e\mathrel{:}\sigma=\tau)$}}\hskip 1.0pt,{\,{a{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\_}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})}_{\,{e}}}=b}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\mathrm{pair^{=}}]{\sim}\big((\sigma,a)=_{\Sigma\,{(\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA}))}{\,{(\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\_}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}))}}}(\tau,b)\big),

where

\varphi\mathrel{:}\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Pi$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(e\mathrel{:}\sigma=\tau)$}}\;{\big(a{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{e}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}=b\xrightarrow{\sim}a{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\_}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})}_{\,{e}}}=b\big)}

is the family of equivalences induced by
Proposition

4.1.4
and
\mathrm{pair^{=}}
is the standard characterization of the equality of
\Sigma
-types,
we get an equivalence

\displaystyle\mathrm{sub}^{=}_{0}\mathrel{:}\big(\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Sigma$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(e\mathrel{:}\sigma=\tau)$}}\hskip 1.0pt,{\,{a{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{e}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}=b}}\big)\xrightarrow{\sim}(\sigma,a)=_{\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA})}(\tau,b)

\displaystyle\mathrm{sub}^{=}_{0}\,(e,{e}^{\prime})\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\mathrm{ap}\,{(\_\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\_)}\,{(\mathrm{pair^{=}}\,(e,\varphi_{e}{e}^{\prime}))}.

Lemma 5.0.1.

\mathsf{p}\upbeta
is a natural isomorphism
.

By definition,
\mathrm{sub}^{=}_{0}\,(\mathrm{refl},\mathrm{refl})\equiv\mathrm{refl}
.
Hence for all substitutions
\sigma,\tau\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA})
,

\mathcal{C}
-terms
a\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})
and
b\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Tm}\,(A{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}})
,
and equalities
e\mathrel{:}\sigma=\tau
and
{e}^{\prime}\mathrel{:}a{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{e}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}=b
,
we have that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}(\sigma\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}(\tau\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0ptb)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}\upbeta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}_{A}\,\mathbin{\ast}\,\mathrm{sub}^{=}_{0}\,(e,{e}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}\upbeta}

canonically commutes by induction on
e
and
{e}^{\prime}
.
Equivalently,

\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\ast}\mathrm{sub}^{=}_{0}\,(e,{e}^{\prime})=\mathsf{p}\upbeta\mathbin{\cdot}e\mathbin{\cdot}{\mathsf{p}\upbeta}^{-1}.

Definition 5.0.2.

\upeta
-equality of substitutions
.

For all
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta
and
\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0pt{A}})
, denote by

\displaystyle\upeta^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\sigma}\mathrel{:}(\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt{{\mathsf{q}_{A}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}}})=\sigma

\displaystyle\upeta^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\sigma}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}{\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathbin{\diamond}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}(\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\upeta\mathbin{\ast}\sigma)\mathbin{\cdot}\lambda

the equality in the first part of the proof of
Lemma

4.3.1
.
This is an
\upeta
-rule for substitutions into extended contexts.

Definition 5.0.3.

Equality of substitutions into extended contexts, revisited
.

Suppose that
\sigma,\tau\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA})
are substitutions into an extended context.
We define an equivalence

\mathrm{sub}^{=}_{\sigma,\tau}\mathrel{:}\big(\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Sigma$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(e\mathrel{:}\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma=\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\diamond}\tau)$}}\hskip 1.0pt,{\,{\mathsf{q}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{e}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}=\mathsf{q}{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}}\big)\xrightarrow{\sim}\sigma=\tau

as follows: if

e\mathrel{:}\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma=\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}\tau

and

{e}^{\prime}\mathrel{:}\mathsf{q}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{e}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}=\mathsf{q}{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}},

then take
\mathrm{sub}^{=}_{\sigma,\tau}\,(e,{e}^{\prime})
to be the composite

\sigma\hskip 4.30554pt{\mathrel{\to}^{{\upeta^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\sigma}}^{-1}}}\hskip 4.30554pt({\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathsf{q}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}})\hskip 4.30554pt{\mathrel{\to}^{\mathrm{sub}^{=}_{0}\,(e,{e}^{\prime\hskip-1.0pt\prime})}}\hskip 4.30554pt({\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\diamond}\tau}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathsf{q}{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}})\hskip 4.30554pt{\mathrel{\to}^{\upeta^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\tau}}}\hskip 4.30554pt\tau,

where
{e}^{\prime\hskip-1.0pt\prime}\mathrel{:}{\mathsf{q}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{e}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}}={\mathsf{q}{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}}}

is canonically constructed from
{e}^{\prime}
.
This definition yields an equivalence, being essentially the composition of
\mathrm{sub}^{=}_{0}
with the equivalence given by path-composing with
{\upeta^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\sigma}}^{-1}
and
\upeta^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\tau}
.

Now, it is natural to ask if a
\upbeta
-rule holds for the first argument of
\mathrm{sub}^{=}_{\sigma,\tau}
, i.e. if, for all
\sigma
and
\tau
, the composition

\big(\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Sigma$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma=\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\diamond}\tau)$}}\hskip 1.0pt,{\,{\mathsf{q}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{\_}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}=\mathsf{q}{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}}\big)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{sub}^{=}_{\sigma,\tau}}\sigma=\tau\xrightarrow{\mathsf{p}\,\mathbin{\ast}\,\_}\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma=\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\diamond}\tau

is equal to the first projection.
By
Lemma

5.0.1
, we calculate that

\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\ast}\mathrm{sub}^{=}_{\sigma,\tau}\,(e,{e}^{\prime})=(\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\ast}{\upeta^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\sigma}}^{-1})\mathbin{\cdot}\mathsf{p}\upbeta\mathbin{\cdot}e\mathbin{\cdot}{\mathsf{p}\upbeta}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}(\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\ast}\upeta^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\tau})

for all
e
and
{e}^{\prime}
.
The desire to have this expression be equal to
e
motivates the next definition, whence
Proposition

5.0.5
immediately follows.

Definition 5.0.4.

Coherators for
\upeta^{\mathrm{sub}}
.

We say that a wild cwf
\mathcal{C}

has coherators for
\upeta^{\mathrm{sub}}
if for all
\Gamma,\Delta\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
,
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta
and
\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA})
, we have that

\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\ast}\upeta^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\sigma}=\mathsf{p}\upbeta

as 2-cells of type
\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}(\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}_{A}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}})=\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma
.

Proposition 5.0.5.

\upbeta
-reduction for
\mathrm{sub}^{=}_{\sigma,\tau}
.

Suppose
\sigma,\tau\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA})
are equal substitutions, witnessed by

e\mathrel{:}\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma=\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}\tau

and

{e}^{\prime}\mathrel{:}\mathsf{q}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{e}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}=\mathsf{q}{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}
.
If
\mathcal{C}
has coherators for
\upeta^{\mathrm{sub}}
, then

\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\ast}\mathrm{sub}^{=}_{\sigma,\tau}\,(e,{e}^{\prime})=e.

In fact, a wild cwf
\mathcal{C}
has coherators for
\upeta^{\mathrm{sub}}
if its category of contexts has triangle coherators (
Definition

2.2.1
), and it further satisfies the following coherence condition:

Definition 5.0.6.

Coherators for context extension
.

A wild cwf
\mathcal{C}

has coherators for context extension
if, for all
\Gamma,\Delta\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
,
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta
and
\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA})
, the following diagrams of equalities commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}(\mathsf{p}_{A}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}_{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}\mathrm{id}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\ast}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\upeta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}\upbeta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}(\mathsf{p}_{A}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}_{A})\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}(\mathsf{p}_{A}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}_{A}))\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}(\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}_{A}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\alpha}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\ast}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathbin{\diamond}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}\upbeta\mathbin{\ast}\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}\upbeta}

Lemma 5.0.7.

If a wild cwf
\mathcal{C}
has triangle coherators as well as coherators for context extension, then it has coherators for
\upeta^{\mathrm{sub}}
.
Thus, the conclusion of
Proposition

5.0.5
also holds if
\mathcal{C}
has coherators for context extension.

Proof.

Having coherators for
\upeta^{\mathrm{sub}}
is equivalent to having the outer boundary of
Diagram

5.0.7.1
commute for all
\Gamma,\Delta\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
,
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta
and
\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA})
.
We show that this holds by pasting together the regions shown in the interior of the diagram, where the topmost interior square is filled by associativity of whiskering (
Proposition

2.1.4
), the rightmost triangle by the triangle coherator, and the regions marked
\circlearrowright
using the coherators for context extension.

Diagram 5.0.7.1:
Coherators for
\upeta^{\mathrm{sub}}
from coherators for context extension.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}(\mathsf{p}_{A}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}_{A})\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}\mathrm{id}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}(\mathsf{p}_{A}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}_{A}))\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}\mathrm{id})\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}(\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}_{A}{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\ast}(\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\upeta\mathbin{\ast}\sigma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\alpha}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\ast}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathbin{\diamond}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\ast}\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\ast}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\upeta)\mathbin{\ast}\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}\upbeta\mathbin{\ast}\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circlearrowright}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho\mathbin{\ast}\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\circlearrowright}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}\upbeta}

∎

Presumably, the coherence conditions for context extension structures would arise out of the universal properties of sufficiently coherent representable presheaves à la a formulation via wild natural models.
In any case, we can now define:

Definition 5.0.8.

Structurally 2-coherent wild cwfs
.

We say that a wild cwf
\mathcal{C}
is
(structurally) 2-coherent
if
\mathcal{C}
has

❂

a 2-coherent wild category of contexts (
Definition

2.2.3
),

❂

type triangulators (
Definition

4.1.8
) and type pentagonators (
Definition

4.1.9
), and

❂

coherators for context extension (
Definition

5.0.6
).

Examples 5.0.9.

2-coherent internal cwfs
.

Any set-level 1-cwf is immediately 2-coherent, and it is straightforward to check that the universe cwfs have type triangulators, type pentagonators and coherators for context extension.

Conjecture 5.0.10.

The container model
.

We also expect that the higher
container model
of Altenkirch and Kaposi
[
AK21
]
is 2-coherent, to be shown by forthcoming work of Damato and Altenkirch
[
DA24
]
.

6.  Context Comprehension in 2-Coherent Wild Cwfs

Central to the 1-categorical semantics of dependent type theory is the recognition that types form a Grothendieck fibration over contexts, and that context extension maps this fibrational structure back into the category of contexts
\mathcal{C}
, by forming a cartesian-morphism-preserving map into the arrow category
{\mathcal{C}}^{\shortrightarrow}
.
This is summed up in the notion of a
comprehension category

[
Jac93
]
, and it is widely known that 1-cwfs are equivalent to full split comprehension categories
[
Bla91
,
ALN24
]
.
In this section, we prove a higher version of this property for 2-coherent wild cwfs.

6.1.  The universal property of context extension

Lemma 6.1.1.

2-coherent substitution in types is weak pullback
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
is a 2-coherent wild cwf,
\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta})
is a substitution,
and
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta
is a
\mathcal{C}
-type.
Then there is a substitution

{\sigma}{}^{\,.\,{A}}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}(\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\mathsf{p}_{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}_{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}})

from
\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}
to
\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA

Proof.

We claim that a mediating substitution is given by

\mu_{\sigma,A}{}_{,\mathfrak{S}}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}(\tau\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}_{A}{\mathsf{[}{\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{\gamma}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}),

where
\mathsf{q}_{A}{\mathsf{[}{\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Tm}\,{A{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{p}_{A}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}
is transported in the family
\mathsf{Tm}_{\,{\mathrm{B}}}
along

A{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{p}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathrel{\to}^{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}A{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\diamond}\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathrel{\to}^{{{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{\gamma}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathrel{\to}^{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}.

For brevity, denote
\mu_{\sigma,A}{}_{,\mathfrak{S}}
by
\mu
.
By

3.1.4
, constructing

is equivalent to constructing witnesses

\delta\mathrel{:}\mathsf{p}_{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\mathbin{\diamond}\mu=\tau

and

\epsilon\mathrel{:}{\sigma}{}^{\,.\,{A}}\mathbin{\diamond}\mu=\varrho

such that

{\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}({\mathsf{p}\upbeta}^{-1}\mathbin{\ast}\mu)\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha=(\sigma\mathbin{\ast}\delta)\mathbin{\cdot}\gamma\mathbin{\cdot}{(\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\ast}\epsilon)}^{-1}.

Let
\delta\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\mathsf{p}\upbeta
.
Using the equivalence
\mathrm{sub}^{=}_{\_\,,\ \_}
(
Definition

5.0.3
), we define

\epsilon\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\mathrm{sub}^{=}_{({\sigma}{}^{\,.\,{A}}\mathbin{\diamond}\mu),\,\varrho}(\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}),

\epsilon_{0}\mathrel{:}\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}{\sigma}{}^{\,.\,{A}}\mathbin{\diamond}\mu=\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\diamond}\varrho

and

\epsilon_{1}\mathrel{:}\mathsf{q}_{A}{\mathsf{[}{{\sigma}{}^{\,.\,{A}}\mathbin{\diamond}\mu}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{\epsilon_{0}}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}=\mathsf{q}_{A}{\mathsf{[}{\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}

are the 2-cells
14

14

a 2-cell.

constructed as follows.

This boundary commutes, since we can fill the interior of the diagram with the following commuting regions:

❂

(1), which commutes straightforwardly,

❂

(2) and (4), which commute by
Proposition

4.1.7
, and

❂

(3) and (5), which are filled by the type pentagonators.

This shows that
\widetilde{e}=\mathrm{refl}
, which completes the proof
\epsilon_{1}
that

\mathsf{q}_{A}{\mathsf{[}{{\sigma}{}^{\,.\,{A}}\mathbin{\diamond}\mu}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{\epsilon_{0}}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}=\mathsf{q}_{A}{\mathsf{[}{\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{\widetilde{e}}}}=\mathsf{q}_{A}{\mathsf{[}{\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}},

and thus also the proof
\epsilon\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\mathrm{sub}^{=}_{({\sigma}{}^{\,.\,{A}}\mathbin{\diamond}\mu),\,\varrho}(\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1})
that

{\sigma}{}^{\,.\,{A}}\mathbin{\diamond}\mu=\varrho.

Finally, what remains is to show that

{\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}({\mathsf{p}\upbeta}^{-1}\mathbin{\ast}\mu)\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha=(\sigma\mathbin{\ast}\delta)\mathbin{\cdot}\gamma\mathbin{\cdot}{(\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\ast}\epsilon)}^{-1}.

But by
Lemma

5.0.7
we have that
{(\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\ast}\epsilon)}^{-1}={\epsilon}^{-1}_{0}
on the right hand side, and the equality then follows by calculation.
∎

Theorem 6.1.2.

2-coherent substitution in types is pullback
.

The weak pullbacks
\mathfrak{P}_{\sigma,\,{A}}
of
Lemma

6.1.1
are pullbacks.

Proof.

By
Lemma

6.1.1
we have that, for any
\mathrm{B}\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
, the map

\displaystyle\mu_{\sigma,A}\mathrel{:}\mathrm{CommSq}_{(\sigma,\mathsf{p}_{A})}(\mathrm{B})\to\mathcal{C}({\mathrm{B}},\hskip 1.5pt{\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0pt{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}})

\displaystyle\mu_{\sigma,A}((\tau,\varrho,\gamma))\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}(\tau\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}_{A}{\mathsf{[}{\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{\gamma}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}})

is a section of the precomposition map
(\mathfrak{P}_{\sigma,\,{A}}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}_{\mathrm{B}}\_)
.
We show that it’s a retraction of the same, and therefore that
(\mathfrak{P}_{\sigma,\,{A}}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}_{\_}\_)
is a family of equivalences.

That is, for
m\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\mathrm{B}},\hskip 1.5pt{\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0pt{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}})
, we want the equality of substitutions

\mu_{\sigma,A}(\mathfrak{P}_{\sigma,\,{A}}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}m)=m.

By
Corollary

4.3.3
and a calculation similar to the one in the proof of
Lemma

6.1.1
we have that

\mu_{\sigma,A}(\mathfrak{P}_{\sigma,\,{A}}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}m)=(\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\diamond}m\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}_{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}{\mathsf{[}{m}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{e}}}),

where

e\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}{({{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{\mathsf{p}\upbeta}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}})}{\mathsf{[}{m}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{{\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}(\mathsf{p}\upbeta\mathbin{\ast}m)\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}.

On the other hand,

m=(\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\diamond}m\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}_{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}{\mathsf{[}{m}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}}}})

by
Lemma

4.3.1
, and thus by
Corollary

4.3.3
again it’s enough to show that
e={{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}
.

By path algebra this amounts to showing the commutativity of a diagram of equalities that looks like the one formed by regions (3), (4) and (5) of
Diagram

6.1.1.1
, but where we replace
\mu
with
m
.
Commutativity of this diagram then follows as in the proof of
Lemma

6.1.1
, i.e. by
Proposition

4.1.7
and type pentagonators.
∎

6.2.  Split 2-coherent wild cwfs

Definition 6.2.1.

Cleavings of wild cwfs
.

A
cleaving
of a wild cwf
\mathcal{C}
is an assignment

\mathfrak{cl}\mathrel{:}\raisebox{-1.43994pt}{$\Pi$}\,{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$(\Gamma,\Delta\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0})\,(\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta}))\,(A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta)$}}\;{\mathrm{Pullback}_{(\sigma,\mathsf{p}_{A})}(\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0pt{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}})}

of pullbacks

\mathfrak{cl}_{{\Gamma},{\Delta}}({\sigma},{A})\hskip 4.30554pt\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\hskip 4.30554pt\hbox to114.9pt{\vbox to53.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 57.44734pt\lower-26.09583pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-57.44734pt}{-21.0764pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -79.49 -29.16)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}{}{}}{}{
{}{}{}}
