\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

to each cospan in
\mathcal{C}
of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}_{A}}
.
We call the component
\mathcal{l}_{{\sigma},{A}}
of the pullback
\mathfrak{cl}_{{\Gamma},{\Delta}}({\sigma},{A})
the
chosen lift of
\sigma
at
A
.

The upshot of
Theorem

6.1.2
is then that every 2-coherent wild cwf has a cleaving

\mathfrak{cl}_{{\Gamma},{\Delta}}({\sigma},{A})\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\mathfrak{P}_{\sigma,\,{A}},

which we call the
type substitution cleaving
.
In particular,
\mathcal{l}_{{\sigma},{A}}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}{\sigma}{}^{\,.\,{A}}
is the chosen lift of a substitution
\sigma\mathrel{:}\mathcal{C}({\Gamma},\hskip 1.5pt{\Delta})
at
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta
.
Observe, additionally, that the vertical legs of the pullbacks chosen by the type substitution cleaving are all display maps.
This allows us to make the following definition.

Definition 6.2.2.

Split 2-coherent cwfs
.

A 2-coherent wild cwf
\mathcal{C}
is called
(coherently) split
if the following equality types are contractible:

1.

For every
\Gamma\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
and
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Gamma
, the type of equalities

(\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0pt{A{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}},\ \mathfrak{P}_{\mathrm{id},\,{A}})=(\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA,\ \mathfrak{I}_{\mathsf{p}_{A}})

of pullbacks on
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}_{A}}
.

2.

For all substitutions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}

and
\mathcal{C}
-types
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta
, the type of equalities

\big(\mathrm{B}\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0pt{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}},\ \mathfrak{P}_{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau,\,{A}}\big)=\big(\mathrm{B}\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0pt{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}},\ {\mathfrak{P}_{\tau,\,{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}\mid{\mathfrak{P}_{\sigma,\,{A}}}\big)

of the pullbacks

\mathfrak{P}_{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau,\,{A}}\equiv\hbox to121.1pt{\vbox to56.97pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 60.54982pt\lower-26.33887pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

on

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{p}_{A}}
.

A split 2-coherent wild cwf may be considered a coherent higher version of a splitting of a full comprehension category.
As is to be expected, set-level internal cwfs are split in the sense of
Definition

6.2.2
.
We now show that
univalent
2-coherent wild cwfs are also split.

Proposition 6.2.3.

Let
e\mathrel{:}A={A}^{\prime}
be an equality of
\mathcal{C}
-types
A,{A}^{\prime}\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Gamma
in a wild cwf
\mathcal{C}
.
Then

\mathrm{idd}(\mathrm{ap}\,{(\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0pt\_)}\,{e})=(\mathsf{p}_{A}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}_{A}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}}_{\,{{e}{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{p}_{A}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}).

Proof.

By induction on
e
it’s enough to show that
\mathrm{idd}(\mathrm{refl}_{\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA})=(\mathsf{p}_{A}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}_{A})
, which holds by
\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\upeta
(
Definition

4.2.1
).
∎

Lemma 6.2.4.

In any 2-coherent wild cwf
\mathcal{C}
,

{\mathrm{id}}{}^{\,.\,{A}}=\mathrm{idd}(\mathrm{ap}\,{(\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0pt\_)}\,{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}})

for any
\Gamma\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
and
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Gamma
.

Proof.

By
Proposition

6.2.3
it’s enough to show that
{\mathrm{id}}{}^{\,.\,{A}}=(\mathsf{p}_{A{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}_{A{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{p}_{A{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}})
, which holds by
Corollary

4.3.3
and the left type triangulator.
∎

In fact, the equality of
Lemma

6.2.4
improves to an equality of pullbacks.
Recall from
Proposition

3.2.4
that the identity commuting square
\mathfrak{I}_{f}
is a pullback for any morphism
f
in a 2-coherent wild category
\mathcal{C}
.

Corollary 6.2.5.

For any context
\Gamma\mathrel{:}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
and
\mathcal{C}
-type
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Gamma
in a 2-coherent wild cwf
\mathcal{C}
, the pullbacks

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{2.72122pt}{-9.314pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 3.77 -12.89)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

are equal elements of
\mathrm{Pullback}((\mathrm{id},\mathsf{p}_{A}))
.

Proof.

We have that

\mathrm{ap}\,{(\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0pt\_)}\,{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}\mathrel{:}\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0pt{A{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}=\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA,

and by
Corollary

3.1.13
it’s enough to show that

\mathfrak{P}_{\mathrm{id},\,{A}}=\mathfrak{I}_{\mathsf{p}_{A}}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}\mathrm{idd}(\mathrm{ap}\,{(\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0pt\_)}\,{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}})
.
But by
Lemma

6.2.4
we may as well show that

\mathfrak{P}_{\mathrm{id},\,{A}}=\mathfrak{I}_{\mathsf{p}_{A}}\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}{\mathrm{id}}{}^{\,.\,{A}},

which holds by
Remark

3.2.5
.
∎

Lemma 6.2.6.

Suppose
\mathcal{C}
is a 2-coherent wild cwf.
For all substitutions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}

and
\mathcal{C}
-types
A\mathrel{:}\mathsf{Ty}\,\Delta
,

(\mathrm{B}\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA{\mathsf{[}{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}},\ \mathfrak{P}_{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau,\,{A}})=(\mathrm{B}\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}},\ {\mathfrak{P}_{\tau,\,{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}\mid{\mathfrak{P}_{\sigma,\,{A}}}).

Proof.

By
Corollary

3.1.13
it’s enough to give

e\mathrel{:}\mathrm{B}\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA{\mathsf{[}{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}=\mathrm{B}\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}

such that

\mathfrak{P}_{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau,\,{A}}=({\mathfrak{P}_{\tau,\,{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}\mid{\mathfrak{P}_{\sigma,\,{A}}})\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}\mathrm{idd}(e).

Take

e\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\mathrm{ap}\,{(\mathrm{B}\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0pt\_)}\,{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}},

then by
Proposition

6.2.3
we may as well show that

\mathfrak{P}_{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau,\,{A}}=({\mathfrak{P}_{\tau,\,{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}\mid{\mathfrak{P}_{\sigma,\,{A}}})\mathbin{\raisebox{0.96002pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\boxempty$}}(\mathsf{p}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{p}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}),

or, equivalently, give three equalities

\displaystyle\delta\mathrel{:}\mathsf{p}_{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}=\mathsf{p}_{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\mathbin{\diamond}(\mathsf{p}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{p}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}),

\displaystyle\epsilon\mathrel{:}{(\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau)}{}^{\,.\,{A}}=({\sigma}{}^{\,.\,{A}}\mathbin{\diamond}{\tau}{}^{\,.\,{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}})\mathbin{\diamond}(\mathsf{p}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{p}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}})

and

\eta\mathrel{:}\mathfrak{p}_{{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau},{A}}=\big((\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau)\mathbin{\ast}\delta\big)\mathbin{\cdot}{\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}\big(({\mathfrak{p}_{{\tau},{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}\mid{\mathfrak{p}_{{\sigma},{A}}})\mathbin{\ast}(\mathsf{p}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{p}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}})\big)\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha\mathbin{\cdot}(\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\ast}{\epsilon}^{-1}),

where
\mathfrak{p}_{{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau},{A}}
,
\mathfrak{p}_{{\tau},{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}
and
\mathfrak{p}_{{\sigma},{A}}
are the 2-cells
{\mathsf{p}\upbeta}^{-1}
of the corresponding pullbacks given by the type substitution cleaving.

Take
\delta\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}{\mathsf{p}\upbeta}^{-1}
.
We will define
\epsilon\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}\mathrm{sub}^{=}(\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1})
, where
\mathrm{sub}^{=}
is the equivalence defined at
Definition

5.0.3
, and where we seek equalities

\epsilon_{0}\mathrel{:}\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\diamond}{(\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau)}{}^{\,.\,{A}}=\mathsf{p}\mathbin{\diamond}({\sigma}{}^{\,.\,{A}}\mathbin{\diamond}{\tau}{}^{\,.\,{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}})\mathbin{\diamond}(\mathsf{p}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{p}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}})

and

\epsilon_{1}\mathrel{:}\mathsf{q}{\mathsf{[}{{(\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau)}{}^{\,.\,{A}}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{\epsilon_{0}}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}=\mathsf{q}{\mathsf{[}{({\sigma}{}^{\,.\,{A}}\mathbin{\diamond}{\tau}{}^{\,.\,{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}})\mathbin{\diamond}(\mathsf{p}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{p}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}})}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}.

Now, from
Proposition

5.0.5
we have that

\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\ast}{\epsilon}^{-1}={(\mathsf{p}_{A}\mathbin{\ast}\epsilon)}^{-1}={\epsilon}^{-1}_{0},

and by rearranging the type of
\eta
we may take

\epsilon_{0}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}{\mathfrak{p}_{{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau},{A}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}\big((\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau)\mathbin{\ast}\delta\big)\mathbin{\cdot}{\alpha}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}\big(({\mathfrak{p}_{{\tau},{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}\mid{\mathfrak{p}_{{\sigma},{A}}})\mathbin{\ast}(\mathsf{p}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{p}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}})\big)\mathbin{\cdot}\alpha.

What remains, then, is to construct
\epsilon_{1}
.
By applying
\mathsf{q}\upbeta
and
{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}
to reduce the generic terms on the left and right, we calculate that its type is equivalent to

\mathsf{q}_{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}\,\mathrm{B}}_{\,{e_{1}}}}=\mathsf{q}_{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{t}}}}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}^{\mathsf{Tm}\,\mathrm{B}}_{\,{e_{2}}}},

where the left and right hand sides are transported, respectively, over equalities

e_{1}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{{\mathsf{p}\upbeta}^{-1}}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{\epsilon_{0}}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}

and

e_{2}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{p}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{{\mathsf{p}\upbeta}^{-1}}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{\widetilde{e}\,}{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{p}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{p}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1},

and where

\widetilde{e}\mathbin{\vcentcolon\equiv}{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{{\mathsf{p}\upbeta}^{-1}}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{\big({{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{{\mathsf{p}\upbeta}^{-1}}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\big)}{\mathsf{[}{{\tau}{}^{\,.\,{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}.

It’s now enough to show that
e_{1}=e_{2}
.
This amounts to giving a filling of
Diagram

6.2.6.1
,
which we divide into three regions filled with coherence cells as shown in
Diagram

6.2.6.2
and
Diagram

6.2.6.3
.

Diagram 6.2.6.1:

The pasting proof of
e_{1}=e_{2}
splits into three regions, which are filled with the cells shown in
Diagram

6.2.6.2
and
Diagram

6.2.6.3
.
We abbreviate the substitution
(\mathsf{p}\hskip 1.5pt\mathord{,}\hskip 2.0pt\mathsf{q}{}_{\,\downarrow}{{}_{\,{{{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}{\mathsf{[}{\mathsf{p}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}})
by
\varrho

and the substitution
{{({{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}^{-1}\mathbin{\cdot}{{}{\mathsf{[}{{}^{=}{{\mathsf{p}\upbeta}^{-1}}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}\mathbin{\cdot}{{\mathsf{[}{\mathbin{\diamond}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}})}{\mathsf{[}{{\tau}{}^{\,.\,{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}{\mathsf{[}{\varrho}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}
by
\xi
.

Filling regions (I) and (II) of
Diagram

6.2.6.1
with coherence cells.

Definition

4.1.9
); those marked (2), by naturality of

(
Proposition

4.1.7
); (3), by associativity of whiskering (
Proposition

2.1.4
); and (4), by the pentagon associator of the category of contexts (
Definition

4.1.9
).

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}

∎

Putting the previous results together, we have:

Theorem 6.2.7.

Split comprehension for set-level and univalent cwfs
.

Any set-level or univalent 2-coherent wild cwf
\mathcal{C}
is coherently split.

Proof.

By
Corollary

6.2.5
and
Lemma

6.2.6
, the equality types

(\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0pt{A{\mathsf{[}{\mathrm{id}}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}},\ \mathfrak{P}_{\mathrm{id},\,{A}})=(\Gamma\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA,\ \mathfrak{I}_{\mathsf{p}_{A}})

and

(\mathrm{B}\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA{\mathsf{[}{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}},\ \mathfrak{P}_{\sigma\mathbin{\diamond}\tau,\,{A}})=(\mathrm{B}\mathord{.}\hskip 1.0ptA{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}{\mathsf{[}{\tau}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}},\ {\mathfrak{P}_{\tau,\,{A{\mathsf{[}{\sigma}\mathsf{]}_{\mathsf{T}}}}}}\mid{\mathfrak{P}_{\sigma,\,{A}}})

are inhabited for all appropriately typed
A
,
\sigma
and
\tau
.
By
Propositions

3.3.2
and
3.3.3
they are propositions when
\mathcal{C}
is set-level, or 2-coherently univalent.
∎

Corollary 6.2.8.

Thus, the syntax cwf QIIT of Altenkirch and Kaposi
[
AK16
]
as well as any univalent universe cwf
\mathcal{U}
is split 2-coherent.

7.  Discussion

We have given a unified account of the cloven fibrational structure of 2-coherent internal models of homotopical dependent type theory, in such a way so as to include set-level models such as the syntax as well as the higher models given by universe types.

Now, the generalized algebraic presentation of 2-coherent wild cwfs straightforwardly yields an internally definable type of
morphisms
of such, which sets up the possibility of internally studying “transfers” of constructions
between internal models; in particular, from the syntax to a universe type.
This is a large part of the motivation of the present work—specifically, the theory developed here is intended to provide a precise formal setting in which to investigate

(1)

the construction of classifiers of semisimplicial and other Reedy fibrant inverse diagrams
[
KS17
]
in (internal models of) homotopical type theory
[
CK21
,
CK24
]
, and

(2)

the relation of this problem to that of the self-interpretation of HoTT
[
Shu14
]
.

Separately from questions of infinite higher coherent constructions, we hope that our theory can still be useful by immediately specializing to yield notions of 1-truncated “2-cwfs”.
For instance, by modifying the definition of a 2-coherent wild cwf to additionally require that

(1)

the category of contexts
\mathcal{C}
is a precategory,

(2)

the presheaf of
\mathcal{C}
-types is valued in 1-types, and

(3)

the presheaf of
\mathcal{C}
-terms is set-valued,

we obtain a simple higher generalization of the notion of a 1-cwf, which conjecturally includes the container higher model of type theory
[
AK21
]
as an instance.
Then via Rezk completion and our results, any instance of such a higher cwf should be equivalent to a split one.
A less naïve approach would be to use univalent bicategory theory
[
Ahr+21
]
, noting
Proposition

2.2.4
, to develop a full theory of 2-cwfs.

As a final remark, we have developed wild categories with families for their anticipated applicability to specific further internal constructions, but we also expect the study of wild natural models and wild comprehension categories to prove complementarily fruitful.
